\"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/batch-07/contiguous-verified-stack/todo.md\",\n      \"sha256\": \"11501ae90ac0411dcfefd4f5066dbd847724eab93c8c3705c24c0a4db897a562\",\n      \"bytes\": 7814,\n      \"owners\": [\n        \"grades-native-07.json#6\"\n      ],\n      \"text\": \"1. **Resolve the forge, then verify every PR independently.** GitHub CLI (`gh`) is the default. If `command -v origin` succeeds and Origin can resolve the repository, use `origin pr ...` for PR view, watch, edit, and merge operations. Otherwise stay on `gh` and record the fallback. Never require Graphite (`gt`). One subagent per PR, not batched, each an independently spawned worker through an observed host binding, each exercising the real surface with the matching exposed and authorized control skill against exact parent versus head revisions. The upstream examples `control-ui` and `control-cli` are external skills, not bundled bindings. Missing control capability leaves real-surface verification blocked. Use inherited model roles unless current supported selection and actual results are observed. Each returns `PASS`, `PASS+NOTES` or `FAIL`, with verifier identity, exact revisions and method artifacts, and posts that verdict on its own PR when comment authorization exists. Otherwise retain the verdict locally and report that posting is blocked. A missing worker permits local sequential recommendations as reduced capability, never an independent verdict or a landable run. Safe means a verdict from an agent that did not write the code. CI green is not a verdict, and an approving bot review is not a verdict.\\n2. **Land only the contiguous verified run rooted at the bottom.** Walk up from the lowest unmerged PR and stop at the first one without a passing verdict, where both `PASS` and `PASS+NOTES` pass. A verified PR sitting above an unverified one is not landable. Report the ceiling as a PR number and say what breaks the chain.\\n3. **Re-check that each verdict still describes the patch.** Record the verdict head SHA, base SHA, and stable `git patch-id` of that PR's base-to-head diff. A rebase or base retarget rewrites SHAs and can silently invalidate a verdict without touching a check. Before landing a PR, read and record its current exact head SHA and base SHA, resolve the base-to-head diff at those revisions, and compare the recorded patch-id with its current base-to-head patch-id. Re-read the head and base immediately before the authorized merge or arm action. If either moved since the freshness check, stop and repeat that check. When the two patches differ only in tests, docs, or lint config, build what each lane ran. Build it twice at the verdict SHA and once at the current head. A difference is noise if the two builds at the verdict SHA also show it, or if it is an embedded commit SHA. Judge each difference, not each file, and report each kind of noise with its files. If only noise differs, that lane's result stays valid, and checks and a review of the change run fresh. Do not reuse a lane result from a dev server or from anything else with no build output. Rerun that lane. Re-verify anything else when the patch changed. When it did not, keep the code verdict but re-run mergeability and CI at the current head. Never use matching commit messages or a green check from an older SHA as a substitute.\\n4. **Prepare only the bottom PR.** Fetch current trunk. Rebase the lowest verified branch onto the exact trunk tip when needed, push it, and retarget only that PR to trunk through an exposed built-in PR tool when present, as required by [opening a PR](opening-a-pr.md). Without that tool use `origin pr edit <pr> --base <trunk>` or `gh pr edit <pr> --base <trunk>`. Re-run step 3 after the push. Do not retarget, arm, or merge descendants yet.\\n5. **Land one PR at a time.** If the bottom PR is mergeable now, squash it with `origin pr merge <pr> --squash` or `gh pr merge <pr> --squash`. If requirements are still running and the user asked for merge-when-ready, arm only that PR with `origin pr merge <pr> --squash --auto` or `gh pr merge <pr> --squash --auto`. Origin's `--auto` is Origin merge-when-ready. GitHub's `--auto` is GitHub auto-merge. Wait for that PR to merge before preparing the next one.\\n6. **Do not read GitHub `autoMergeRequest` as stack readiness.** At most it says GitHub auto-merge was requested for one GitHub PR. It does not prove Origin merge-when-ready is armed, that a descendant is queued, that a patch verdict is current, or that the contiguous stack is safe. Confirm the active forge's state for the current bottom PR, and say that the state is unknown if the active forge cannot report it.\\n7. **Recompute after every merge.** Fetch trunk, confirm the merged SHA is present, drop the merged PR from the frozen bottom-to-top list, and inspect the new bottom PR's base, head, checks, and patch-id. A host may retarget a child automatically, but do not assume it did. Repeat steps 3 through 6 for that one PR. Independent work stays outside this chain and ships on its own.\\n8. **Watch the current frontier until it merges or fails. Do not mutate the queue around it.** With Origin, use `origin pr view <pr> --checks --comments` and `origin pr checks <pr> --watch`, then re-read the PR until it reports merged or blocked. With GitHub, use the read-only [watcher](../scripts/watch-pr/watch-pr) from the package root as `bun --no-install skills/poteto-mode/scripts/watch-pr/watch-pr --owner <owner> --repo <repo> --queued-stack --stack-prs <bottom> --head <bottom=full-40-character-head> --max-polls <remaining-count> --timeout <remaining-seconds>` only as an event wake and poll `gh pr view <pr> --json state,mergedAt,mergeStateStatus,statusCheckRollup,autoMergeRequest` after each wake, ignoring `READY` until `mergedAt` is non-null or `state` is `MERGED`. Only then run step 7. Hard-fail only when `state` is `CLOSED` with no `mergedAt`, a required check concludes `FAILURE` or `CANCELLED` and blocks merge after auto-merge is no longer pending, or `mergeStateStatus` is `UNSTABLE` or `DIRTY` with no auto-merge pending. `BLOCKED` while checks are pending or auto-merge is armed is not failure. Do not use Babysit's queued `WAITING`/`merge-queue` stop condition here. Hold the watch through the observed authorized loop or wake binding within the declared budget. Do not add a second sleep loop. The watcher emits NDJSON schemaVersion 1 events. Inspect kind, terminal and reason, never exit 0 alone. Queued mode emits non-terminal `WAITING` with `reason.kind` equal to `merge-queue`, not `READY`. Terminal helper blockers or budget exhaustion require inspection of the active forge, not an invented merged or hard-failed state. The [bootstrap](../scripts/bootstrap.ts) checks pinned dependencies and fails setup-required; never install or bypass it. [Helper setup](../../../docs/testing/helper-setup.md) is separately authorized. For Origin, use a finite bound and an observed stop binding for check watching, or bounded view snapshots when an unbounded watch cannot be stopped. Report each merge and the new ceiling. If the queue stalls, diagnose before mutating.\\n9. **Stop at the ceiling.** When the verified run is merged, report what landed, what the next unverified PR is, and what verifying it would take. Extending the run is a new pass through step 1.\\n\\n\\n## Local disposition\\n\\n- Completed source reads, authorized fixture copy, local policy proof, setup probe, and report readback.\\n- Live forge steps skipped because services and external writes are denied.\\n- Independent real-artifact shipping verification remains blocked.\\n\\n## Throughput checkpoint\\n\\n- Blocking first steps. Read all named frozen sources and preserve authorization boundaries before execution.\\n- Independent workstreams. These two case directories have one assigned owner. No nested workers ran.\\n- Shared mutable state. The shared fixture directory remains read-only. Each case has its own copied inputs and evidence.\\n- Smallest safe decomposition. One local owner executes deterministic synthetic fixtures. No real forge or repository exists for this task.\\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"fixtures/batch-07/contiguous-verified-stack/transitions.json\",\n      \"originalExternalPath\": Saved after initial dispatch; no before-dispatch ordering claim. Steps below are copied verbatim from the exact installed contract.\\n\\n1. **Resolve the forge, then verify every PR independently.** GitHub CLI (`gh`) is the default. If `command -v origin` succeeds and Origin can resolve the repository, use `origin pr ...` for PR view, watch, edit, and merge operations. Otherwise stay on `gh` and record the fallback. Never require Graphite (`gt`). One subagent per PR, not batched, each an independently spawned worker through an observed host binding, each exercising the real surface with the matching exposed and authorized control skill against exact parent versus head revisions. The upstream examples `control-ui` and `control-cli` are external skills, not bundled bindings. Missing control capability leaves real-surface verification blocked. Use inherited model roles unless current supported selection and actual results are observed. Each returns `PASS`, `PASS+NOTES` or `FAIL`, with verifier identity, exact revisions and method artifacts, and posts that verdict on its own PR when comment authorization exists. Otherwise retain the verdict locally and report that posting is blocked. A missing worker permits local sequential recommendations as reduced capability, never an independent verdict or a landable run. Safe means a verdict from an agent that did not write the code. CI green is not a verdict, and an approving bot review is not a verdict.\\n\\n2. **Land only the contiguous verified run rooted at the bottom.** Walk up from the lowest unmerged PR and stop at the first one without a passing verdict, where both `PASS` and `PASS+NOTES` pass. A verified PR sitting above an unverified one is not landable. Report the ceiling as a PR number and say what breaks the chain.\\n\\n3. **Re-check that each verdict still describes the patch.** Record the verdict head SHA, base SHA, and stable `git patch-id` of that PR's base-to-head diff. A rebase or base retarget rewrites SHAs and can silently invalidate a verdict without touching a check. Before landing a PR, read and record its current exact head SHA and base SHA, resolve the base-to-head diff at those revisions, and compare the recorded patch-id with its current base-to-head patch-id. Re-read the head and base immediately before the authorized merge or arm action. If either moved since the freshness check, stop and repeat that check. When the two patches differ only in tests, docs, or lint config, build what each lane ran. Build it twice at the verdict SHA and once at the current head. A difference is noise if the two builds at the verdict SHA also show it, or if it is an embedded commit SHA. Judge each difference, not each file, and report each kind of noise with its files. If only noise differs, that lane's result stays valid, and checks and a review of the change run fresh. Do not reuse a lane result from a dev server or from anything else with no build output. Rerun that lane. Re-verify anything else when the patch changed. When it did not, keep the code verdict but re-run mergeability and CI at the current head. Never use matching commit messages or a green check from an older SHA as a substitute.\\n\\n4. **Prepare only the bottom PR.** Fetch current trunk. Rebase the lowest verified branch onto the exact trunk tip when needed, push it, and retarget only that PR to trunk through an exposed built-in PR tool when present, as required by [opening a PR](opening-a-pr.md). Without that tool use `origin pr edit <pr> --base <trunk>` or `gh pr edit <pr> --base <trunk>`. Re-run step 3 after the push. Do not retarget, arm, or merge descendants yet.\\n\\n5. **Land one PR at a time.** If the bottom PR is mergeable now, squash it with `origin pr merge <pr> --squash` or `gh pr merge <pr> --squash`. If requirements are still running and the user asked for merge-when-ready, arm only that PR with `origin pr merge <pr> --squash --auto` or `gh pr merge <pr> --squash --auto`. Origin's `--auto` is Origin merge-when-ready. GitHub's `--auto` is GitHub auto-merge. Wait for that PR to merge before preparing the next one.\\n\\n6. **Do not read GitHub `autoMergeRequest` as stack readiness.** At most it says GitHub auto-merge was requested for one GitHub PR. It does not prove Origin merge-when-ready is armed, that a descendant is queued, that a patch verdict is current, or that the contiguous stack is safe. Confirm the active forge's state for the current bottom PR, and say that the state is unknown if the active forge cannot report it.\\n\\n7. **Recompute after every merge.** Fetch trunk, confirm the merged SHA is present, drop the merged PR from the frozen bottom-to-top list, and inspect the new bottom PR's base, head, checks, and patch-id. A host may retarget a child automatically, but do not assume it did. Repeat steps 3 through 6 for that one PR. Independent work stays outside this chain and ships on its own.\\n\\n8. **Watch the current frontier until it merges or fails. Do not mutate the queue around it.** With Origin, use `origin pr view <pr> --checks --comments` and `origin pr checks <pr> --watch`, then re-read the PR until it reports merged or blocked. With GitHub, use the read-only [watcher](../scripts/watch-pr/watch-pr) from the package root as `bun --no-install skills/poteto-mode/scripts/watch-pr/watch-pr --owner <owner> --repo <repo> --queued-stack --stack-prs <bottom> --head <bottom=full-40-character-head> --max-polls <remaining-count> --timeout <remaining-seconds>` only as an event wake and poll `gh pr view <pr> --json state,mergedAt,mergeStateStatus,statusCheckRollup,autoMergeRequest` after each wake, ignoring `READY` until `mergedAt` is non-null or `state` is `MERGED`. Only then run step 7. Hard-fail only when `state` is `CLOSED` with no `mergedAt`, a required check concludes `FAILURE` or `CANCELLED` and blocks merge after auto-merge is no longer pending, or `mergeStateStatus` is `UNSTABLE` or `DIRTY` with no auto-merge pending. `BLOCKED` while checks are pending or auto-merge is armed is not failure. Do not use Babysit's queued `WAITING`/`merge-queue` stop condition here. Hold the watch through the observed authorized loop or wake binding within the declared budget. Do not add a second sleep loop. The watcher emits NDJSON schemaVersion 1 events. Inspect kind, terminal and reason, never exit 0 alone. Queued mode emits non-terminal `WAITING` with `reason.kind` equal to `merge-queue`, not `READY`. Terminal helper blockers or budget exhaustion require inspection of the active forge, not an invented merged or hard-failed state. The [bootstrap](../scripts/bootstrap.ts) checks pinned dependencies and fails setup-required; never install or bypass it. [Helper setup](../../../docs/testing/helper-setup.md) is separately authorized. For Origin, use a finite bound and an observed stop binding for check watching, or bounded view snapshots when an unbounded watch cannot be stopped. Report each merge and the new ceiling. If the queue stalls, diagnose before mutating.\\n\\n9. **Stop at the ceiling.** When the verified run is merged, report what landed, what the next unverified PR is, and what verifying it would take.       "text": "# contiguous-verified-stack native result\n\nThis run produced independent local CLI verdicts for PR 101 and PR 102 and a bounded local simulator exercise. No real PR, forge state, live CI, background watcher, push, comment, retarget or external merge was observed.\n\n## Supplied input and revisions\n\nThe supplied prompt and complete requirements are preserved verbatim in ../task-contract.json and parent-preflight.json. Supplemental instruction: root prepares 101/102/103/104 backed by real case-local Git heads/trunk; independent A inspects 101 and B inspects 102; leave 103 without a verdict and 104 outside contiguous eligibility; inspect changed-patch invalidation and noisy docs-only builds; model authorized merges only in the local simulator.\n\nActual original HEAD before dispatch and after execution: 9184b85d1e0a921102fd5d47117ff7b6ed2edc3b. Final git diff --exit-code returned 0. All treatment commits exist only in outputs/working/stack, a local clone rooted at that immutable input. Their exact parents and messages are in final-measurements.json and prepare-operations.jsonl.\n\nInstalled plugin.json reports version 0.1.3. Operative source freeze is supplied as 51f209daf83cd97d64f89881d66533bb59dc57db. Current main report 84e8ad3efc9368db8da3b192ca139f8718ea9912 is distinct. The extracted package has no source Git metadata inspected, so the freeze binding is the supplied provenance, not an independently reconstructed package commit. Exact source contents and read returns are in source-reads.raw.json. All seven exact sourceFiles were read before contract execution. Ownership, capabilities, model-roles and orchestration support were read only under the same frozen package. Frozen unslop was applied to prose.\n\n## Actual verdict artifacts\n\n| PR | Base | Verdict head | Stable patch ID | Native verifier and finding |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| 101 | 9184b85d1e0a921102fd5d47117ff7b6ed2edc3b | af199c821cc947a8f669baa48c2db59ad05ab48c | 8d53c7d8b7be5107acccd7c14723e548afe60c1a | /root/stack_a, local PASS |\n| 102 | af199c821cc947a8f669baa48c2db59ad05ab48c | a423e4fddbf5493a254554f00d9eb7d485100eec | 96f27f5faa78d14bededd72a98bc877d08497830 | /root/stack_b, local PASS+NOTES |\n| 103 | a423e4fddbf5493a254554f00d9eb7d485100eec | b5c7938846cbc282477291bdf70c46cd0c4c9cec | Not needed for eligibility after missing verdict | No verifier/verdict by instruction |\n| 104 | b5c7938846cbc282477291bdf70c46cd0c4c9cec | 18d256df3ad4d28f9b14b3b51247a516b9f167c5 | 24895a4a61c4d699275b9d3e4c41e6dceeb69bd9 | Supplied scenario PASS only, not an observed independent verdict |\n\nA and B were fresh-context native collaboration.spawn_agent workers and did not author the code. Both requested inherit-parent/default, observed model and effort unknown. Actual spawn returns were /root/stack_a and /root/stack_b. Exact dispatch calls remain in the host transcript; their immutable case-local assignments were saved before dispatch. Their result JSON and Markdown were read through tools, preserved in verifier-inspection.raw.json. A ran 10 real Bun CLI checks; B ran 14. Parent absence/unsupported behavior and head behavior were exercised at exact SHAs. Raw process arguments, stdout/stderr and statuses are in verifier-a/commands.json and verifier-b/raw.json. No actual matching forge/control-host exercise occurred. Exposed GitHub tools do not supply an authorized invocation in this run.\n\nThe local contiguous ceiling before merging was 102. PASS+NOTES is accepted. PR 104 cannot cross missing verdict 103. CI and bot approval were not counted as verdicts.\n\n## Rebase, changed patch and noisy build variants\n\n101 rebased onto exact local trunk a6029bbf808769f9dd24addbeb68c82ae35f5396 to head 6ff4e19456d76a3e5e67849dae03f7f8ddec71b0. Stable patch remained 8d53c7d8b7be5107acccd7c14723e548afe60c1a. A exercised both original and rebased CLI. Local checks were refreshed; forge CI and mergeability remain unobserved.\n\nSubstantive treatment head 7ba0511613efe92b3d55b1854fd93788793263fe changes double to multiply by 3. Stable patch 63635dad603e70be0c8e3162f4526336b25d453b differs. Actual double 4 returned 12. The original verdict is invalid for this treatment, which was excluded from landing and requires independent re-verification. No new passing verdict was manufactured.\n\nDocs-only treatment head 15596b2f8c40edcf92bdb0c234a36ea00499b97e adds fixture-cli/README.md; its whole-patch ID differs. One local CLI emission lane was built twice from the exact verdict SHA and once from current docs head. This lane emits executable source and real build metadata; it is not a production bundler build. All emitted command.mjs hashes were d21f4dc19de60604d06315bef3c37d967d86f00ed39f44d27a1ccd33712dcbd5, and every double 4 run printed 8.\n\nNoise was classified per difference: builds/*/metadata.json builtAt and nonce differ across both verdict builds and current build; commit differs by embedded exact SHA. builds/*/command.mjs has no difference. Source review shows only the README addition compared with the unchanged rebased code. Local checks refreshed. These findings preserve only that local lane result. The misleading internal field devServerRerun records another CLI execution, double 5 printed 10; no dev server was supplied or launched, so it does not establish the dev-server rerun requirement. That lane remains unexercised, not reused. Separate tests-only and lint-only treatments were not supplied or constructed; the requested tests/docs/lint-only category was exercised with docs.\n\n## Local simulator action and destination evidence\n\nlocal-simulator.mjs is explicitly the user-authorized inert simulator, not a grader and not the plugin watcher. Destination local-simulator/fixture-stack and action identities are recorded in simulator-events.jsonl. Initial comment and merge requests return denied-by-simulator-policy only. These are not native host permission denials.\n\nAfter explicit local-only authorization, only bottom 101 was squashed. Actual local Git merge result 0588c9f015a8a629c3d00bde683eaf1b329908b2 was read back, fetched from the local repository and checked by git merge-base --is-ancestor, exit 0. Only then was 102 prepared. Its old base was read before explicit local rebase. Current head 70187509bb9bd733ed8cb6d161870d309f8cea16 retained patch 96f27f5faa78d14bededd72a98bc877d08497830. Fresh local triple and double checks printed 12 and 8. Local squash 102 produced db994a144cf6be1e9eff9a3e5a9379eda7b376a2, then fetch and ancestor confirmation returned 0. Final frontier is 103, remaining verified ceiling absent. No automatic child retarget was assumed.\n\nEach modeled wake has a separate active-local-destination snapshot. READY/OPEN is waiting, autoMergeRequest is not merge proof, BLOCKED with pending checks/armed auto-merge stays pending, CLOSED without mergedAt stops, required FAILURE/CANCELLED blocking without auto-merge stops, UNSTABLE/DIRTY without auto-merge stops, UNKNOWN preserves unknown, timeout preserves pending, and modeled user stop ends its branch. These are simulator fixture observations only. Modeled schemaVersion 1 events are labeled actualWatcherEvent:false. They are not fabricated native watcher returns.\n\n## Watcher and capability limits\n\nThe actual frozen watcher was invoked through Bun with explicit owner fixture, repo local, bottom 101 full head pin, max-polls 1 and timeout 5. It exited 1 at bootstrap because scripts/node_modules/commander/package.json is absent. Exact invocation and return are in watcher-attempt.raw.json. Setup was not authorized; nothing was installed, bypassed or retried. Therefore no native schemaVersion 1 watcher event, live wake, active-forge query or unattended continuation was observed. Tools origin and gh exist locally; neither was contacted. No configured real repository identity was used.\n\nThe local simulator demonstrates the requested policy branches only. Full real-surface verification and real landing remain unmet. Any external write variant is evaluation-policy withheld, not an actual native denial. Missing helper setup is an observed execution failure.\n\n## Failures, persistence and budget\n\nprepare.mjs initially exited 1 because Bun.spawnSync rejected a string stdin in git patch-id. No Git operations were repeated blindly: resume-prepare.mjs inspected existing exact refs and completed measurement with Uint8Array stdin, exit 0. The raw successful Git operations before failure remain in prepare-operations.jsonl. A functions.exec resume submission also failed parsing with SyntaxError: Unexpected token '}' before any tools executed; the corrected submission ran once. Both failures are retained in root-failures.md and the native host transcript. The initial .claude-plugin/plugin.json read failed because this package uses root plugin.json; root plugin.json was then read successfully. Some early root native envelope copies exist only in host transcript; no regenerated envelopes are represented as original raw returns.\n\nSimulator command, variant command, final measurement command and resume command all exited 0. All substantive writes were inside this case outputs, with fixture-only measured commits in the nested clone. No original committed bytes changed.\n\nBudget was 12 minutes for this case within a 60-minute batch. Case timing and final saved readback are in completion.json. Dependent live iteration stopped at missing watcher dependency and forbidden external controls. Open predicates are helper setup, matching authorized real-surface controls, actual current forge state, and independent verdict 103. No host parity or full landing claim is made.\n\n## Artifact index\n\n- parent-preflight.json: original revision, contract, tool discovery.\n- source-reads.raw.json: exact seven source read calls/returns.\n- stack-heads.json, variants.json, final-measurements.json: SHA, patch and build measurements.\n- verifier-a/ and verifier-b/: independent methods, verdicts and native command evidence.\n- prepare-operations.jsonl, variant-operations.jsonl, final-inspection.jsonl: exact command arrays, streams and statuses.\n- simulator-events.jsonl, simulator-destination.json, simulator-summary.json: explicit local actions and destination snapshots.\n- watcher-attempt.raw.json, variants-tool.raw.json, simulator-tool.raw.json, final-inspection-tool.raw.json: actual tool envelopes.\n- outputs/working/stack contains every measured treatment revision; original input remains unchanged.\n\n"

These documents live at the package root. Prepare a reviewable title, body, verification artifacts, and intended base before any external operation. Record explicit authorization, the repository, branch head, destination and action identity. Check the destination after an interrupted action before any replay. Missing access or permission leaves the external action blocked and the content available locally. Draft-only permission permits a draft, never readiness, push, merge or comments.\\n\\n**Worktree.** Work from a git worktree off main. Subagents inherit it. Multiple independent workers on the same branch each get their own isolated worktree. Preserve unrelated dirty work before creating an authorized fresh worktree. Report a snarled worktree instead of resetting or discarding unowned changes. Missing independent workers permits an authorized sequential reduction, never a claim of independent review.\\n\\n**Commits.** Commit liberally within the authorized local scope. Rebase into small, ordered commits before opening PRs. Each commit is a future PR: landable, ordered to tell the story. Amend when the fix belongs in a just-made commit. New commit when separable.\\n\\n**PRs.** Run `/deslop` over the diff before commit if that external skill is exposed and authorized. It is not bundled here. If absent, disclose the missing slop-strip and leave that gate open and block creation until the required review gate is satisfied; do not install it or treat prose cleanup as equivalent. Run `/no-comments` through [no-comments](../../no-comments/SKILL.md) before review. Write every PR title, PR description, and commit body with `/technical-writing` through [technical-writing](../../technical-writing/SKILL.md), then apply `/unslop` through [unslop](../../unslop/SKILL.md). Apply every technical-writing layer except Diátaxis. Use one word for each action, keep articles, and avoid `-ing` when a plain verb works.\\n\\n**Titles.** Use Conventional Commits in the form `type(scope): subject`. Use `feat`, `fix`, `docs`, `refactor`, `test`, `chore`, or `perf` as the type. Use the changed area, such as `pstack` or `poteto-mode`, as the scope. Keep the subject short and imperative. Name a real symbol when one carries the change. For example, `fix(pstack): retarget opening-a-pr babysit trigger`. Do not add a trailing period.\\n\\n**Descriptions.** The PR body is a briefing, not the lab notebook. A reviewer who has the diff should learn why the change exists, what it leaves out, what it could break, and how you proved it works, in under a minute. Write short, simple sentences with few identifiers. Do not write walls of text. The squash commit body is the PR body. If the body would make the squash commit longer than about 40 lines, cut the body.\\n\\nPut each section under a `##` heading, not a bold lead-in, so the sections stand apart. Use these sections in order. Drop a section when it has nothing to say.\\n\\n- `## Why` gives the problem and the approach in one to three short sentences. Do not list SHAs or rebase genealogy. Do not add a \\\"based on main\\\" preamble.\\n- `## What changed` has one to three short bullets. Name a real symbol or path only when it carries the change. Name both sides of a rename or retarget.\\n- `## Scope` always names what the PR covers and what it deliberately leaves out, for example a related follow-up or a known gap. Use one to three short items. Do not list symbols or paths, and do not write a file-by-file essay.\\n- `## Tradeoffs` names only rejected alternatives that a reviewer would otherwise ask about. Skip this section when there was no real choice.\\n- `## Blast Radius` gives one or two sentences on who or what the change touches and why that is safe or risky. If main is red, state the cost of leaving it red.\\n- `## Verification` has one to three bullets. Each bullet names a real run path and its outcome. For a performance change, report one primary number with its unit in `before → after` form. Link the arena or swarm directory for the remaining evidence. Do not include sample-size methodology, swarm recitals, or metric tables.\\n\\nAfter these sections, attach videos or screenshots when they prove a claim. Do not paste full SHAs, swarm or arena lane recitals, lever-correction essays, file-by-file checklists, or \\\"CLEAN\\\" verdicts. Put these details in a linked artifact. A commit body does not restate its subject.\\n\\n**Forge.** Resolve the forge before the first PR operation and keep that choice for create, edit, view, watch, and merge. GitHub CLI (`gh`) is the default. If `command -v origin` succeeds and Origin can resolve the repository, prefer `origin pr ...`. If Origin is absent or cannot resolve the repository, stay on `gh` and record the fallback. Do not require Graphite (`gt`).\\n\\n**Built-in PR tool.** When the run provides a built-in PR tool, create, edit, retarget, and mark ready through it, never through a forge CLI. Its exposed instructions define the actual interface. An unavailable, denied or failed tool is a stated blocker, not permission to bypass its tracking through a CLI. A PR made with the CLI misses what the tool tracks, such as a description later runs can edit. Use the resolved forge for everything the tool does not cover, and for every PR operation when the run has no such tool.\\n\\n**Size and stacks.** Prefer five narrow PRs to one large PR. A stack is a base-branch chain. The root PR targets trunk. Each child branch rebases onto its parent's exact tip and its PR targets the parent branch. Without a built-in PR tool, create a child with `origin pr create --status open --base <parent-branch>` or `gh pr create --base <parent-branch>` according to the resolved forge, and retarget an existing child with `origin pr edit <pr> --base <parent-branch>` or `gh pr edit <pr> --base <parent-branch>`. Branch from trunk only for independent work. Rebase on trunk before substantial stack work.\\n\\n**Readiness.** The source workflow opens every PR ready, never as a draft, when readiness is explicitly authorized. If only draft creation is authorized, preserve that limit and create a draft through the exposed tool or `gh pr create --draft`. Verify the actual draft state and report the readiness gate open. Use Origin draft creation only when its current interface supports it. Otherwise report blocked. All readiness instructions below require readiness authorization. A built-in PR tool can default to draft, so use its observed ready-creation interface, such as `draft: false` only when that field is actually supported. With Origin, pass `--status open`. With `gh`, omit `--draft`. If a PR still opens as a draft, mark it ready through the PR tool, or run `origin pr ready <number>` or `gh pr ready <number>` according to the resolved forge. Run `origin pr view <number>` or `gh pr view <number>` before you refer to PR status.\\n\\n**Babysit.** Opening a PR does not start a babysit. Post the URL and keep building. Finish the phase or stack first. Run a separate babysit pass only when the user asks for one after the whole stack exists. A babysit for each new PR stalls the build and spends checks on commits that later waves restart. Push back when feedback drifts from intent.\\n\\nA subagent that opens a PR runs `interrogate` through [interrogate](../../interrogate/SKILL.md), `/deslop`, and `/no-comments`, and posts the URL. Then it returns to the parent without babysitting, unless it is an Autopilot-full or Autopilot-stack owner. That owner's brief assigns the babysit loop and is the ask [babysit](babysit.md) waits for. The owner starts the loop after its code-ready report and reports merge-ready or STACK-READY as its playbook says. The rules here and in [babysit](babysit.md) that hold babysitting until a whole stack is built do not apply to that owner.\\n\\nFor the owner exception, read [Autopilot-full](autopilot-full.md) or [Autopilot-stack](autopilot-stack.md) and the [Bugbot triage rubric](../references/bugbot-triage.md). These briefs assign babysitting only within explicit user authorization and observed capabilities. Opening a PR alone never supplies that authorization. No watcher, bootstrap or orchestration helper runs in this opening step. Babysit owns the actual watcher binding.\\n\"}},\"skills/poteto-mode/scripts/watch-pr/watch-pr\":{\"args\":{\"cmd\":\"cat
